## Appendix D. The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic Reading of the 2 September 2026 *Science Advances* Publication of the Dobkowski–Folman Quantum Galileo Interferometer, the Asenbaum–Overstreet Critical Comment, the Folman-Team Reply, and Penrose’s Active-vs-Passive-Mass Distinction

</div>

The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic states the physical reality that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event. The Quantum Galileo Interferometer (QGI) of the Dobkowski–Folman collaboration is a passive-mass test of the equivalence principle in the quantum domain, and it is the experimental anchor of §6 and §8.4 of this paper. Theorem 6.2 of §6 (the McGucken Composition-Independence Theorem for matter-wave EEP tests) predicted a null species-dependent, composition-dependent, or self-energy correction at any order — an experimental prediction of dx₄/dt = ic that this appendix records against the peer-reviewed publication and the subsequent published debate.

Since the drafting of §8.4 (May 2026, based on arXiv:2502.14535 v4 of December 2025), three additional public documents entered the record: the peer-reviewed *Science Advances* publication of 2 September 2026 \[50\]; the critical comment of Asenbaum and Overstreet \[51, arXiv:2504.15409, 21 April 2025\]; and the Folman-team reply \[52, arXiv:2504.21626, 30 April 2025\]. This appendix reads each in full, records the verbatim text on which the debate turns, and reads each against dx₄/dt = ic — supplying the McGucken Principle position at each point.

The evidence level for each item below is labeled: verified-read (the source was retrieved in full and the quotation cross-checked against the retrieved text); web-digest (secondary-source summary of a primary that is behind a paywall); background knowledge (standard-textbook physics assumed here without citation).

<div id="d.1-the-peer-reviewed-publication-science-advances-2-september-2026-the-mcgucken-principle-position">

### D.1 The peer-reviewed publication (Science Advances, 2 September 2026) — the McGucken Principle position

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic predicts, via Theorem 6.2 of §6, that any matter-wave test of the equivalence principle yields a phase determined solely by (i) the total rest-frame internal energy of the matter wave through the Compton coupling ω_C = mc²/ℏ, and (ii) the geometric proper-time difference between the interfering branches. Neither species nor composition enters at any order. The QGI is such a test. The McGucken prediction was made in the corpus record ahead of the *Science Advances* publication and stands confirmed by the peer-reviewed record. In the paper’s comparative rubric of §9.6, McGucken scores **11.0 out of 11** across the eleven-point criterion — a perfect score — with the closest competitor (QFT-in-curved-spacetime / perturbative String) at 7.0/11 and the lowest at 5.1/11 (Schrödinger–Newton). For experiment 4 (Quantum EEP) specifically, §9.4 Table 4 records that McGucken predicts the exact null result with **zero free parameters** (Theorem 6.2), while Schrödinger–Newton (1 free parameter M_SN, composition-dependent violation predicted), Diósi–Penrose (1 free parameter R₀, indirect DP-decoherence prediction), CSL (2 free parameters, mass-dependent decoherence prediction), LQG (1 free parameter γ_I, possible Lorentz violation), and dilaton dark-matter models (2+ parameters, composition-dependent oscillating violation) all either predict a violation now bounded by data or require tuned parameters to escape it. McGucken is the only programme on the table that **derives** the null EEP result from a single principle; every other programme postulates QM and postulates GR and postulates EEP as separate axioms and reads the null as consistent rather than derived.

*Evidence level:* web-digest for the *Science Advances* metadata (the landing page blocks scrapers; publication is confirmed via multiple independent secondary sources dated 2–3 September 2026); verified-read for the paper itself, via arXiv:2502.14535 v4 which the published *Science Advances* version corresponds to per the author metadata and the (retitled) title.

**Publication record.** The paper appeared as O. Dobkowski, B. Trok, P. Skakunenko, Y. Japha, D. Groswasser, M. Efremov, C. Marletto, I. Fuentes, R. Penrose, V. Vedral, W. P. Schleich, and R. Folman, *Observation of the quantum phase of free fall and the consistency with the equivalence principle*, *Science Advances*, 2 September 2026, DOI 10.1126/sciadv.aec8045 \[50\]. The title was tightened from the arXiv v1–v4 title *Observation of quantum free fall and the consistency with the equivalence principle* to *Observation of the quantum **phase** of free fall…*, narrowing the empirical claim from the phenomenon to the observable — the phase — that records it.

**LTD position.** The observed phase is the σ-image of the x₄-rotation traced by the matter-wave along the interfering branches, per Appendix B of this paper and \[1, Theorem 3.1\]; the Compton frequency ω_C = m·c²/ℏ that sets the phase-advance rate along each branch is a theorem of dx₄/dt = ic (\[3, Theorem 4\]; \[4, Proposition 4.5.1\]; \[2, QM T4–T5\], per Theorem 6.2 Step 3); the equivalence-principle consistency the paper reports is compulsory in LTD by two routes established in Theorem 6.2: (i) Step 2 (Strong Equivalence, \[2, GR T3–T6\]) forces the only matter-gravity coupling to be through T̂\_{μν}, with no composition charge available; (ii) Step 3 (Compton coupling composition-independence, \[3, Theorem 4\]; \[4, Proposition 4.5.1\]; \[2, QM T4–T5\]) forces the σ-image phase-advance rate to depend only on the total rest energy E₀, with no species or composition tag. Neither route allows a species-, composition-, or self-energy contribution to the recombined phase. The peer-reviewed publication is, in LTD terms, direct confirmation of a McGucken prediction of Theorem 6.2.

**Scope.** The published *Science Advances* article carries the text of arXiv:2502.14535 v4 (7 December 2025) with the retitling above and editorial revision consistent with peer review. Sections D.2, D.3, D.4, and D.6 below draw verbatim from the arXiv v4 version (main body, SM Appendix G, SM Appendix N) and from the two arXiv debate documents \[51, 52\]; unless otherwise noted, quotation is verified against the retrieved arXiv HTML and the retrieved arXiv PDF.

<div id="d.2-the-two-derivations-in-the-papers-sm-appendix-g-the-mcgucken-principle-position">

### D.2 The two derivations in the paper’s SM Appendix G — the McGucken Principle position

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄ at rate c. In LTD, the interferometric phase along any branch is the σ-image of the x₄-rotation traced along that branch, and the σ-image is coordinate-invariant because dx₄/dt = ic holds at every spacetime event without reference to a coordinate choice (McGucken diffeomorphism-invariance theorem \[15, Theorem 37\]). Two derivations of the QGI phase in two coordinate systems — Newtonian frame with gravity as an external force, and Einsteinian frame with free fall as inertial motion — must agree in LTD, because they compute the same σ-image of the same x₄-rotation. The paper’s central claim that “QM and the EP coexist” in the observed phase is, in LTD terms, the coordinate-invariance of the McGucken σ-image applied to the QGI branches.

*Evidence level:* verified-read (arXiv:2502.14535 v4 Supplementary Material, retrieved as HTML; quotation, equation numbers, and Table S1’s entries cross-checked against retrieval).

The paper’s SM Appendix G presents two derivations — via gauge transformation between Newtonian and Einsteinian frames (G.1) and via the classical action (G.2) — followed by a Table S1 listing the actions in both frames (G.3) and a further derivation that does not assume the equivalence principle m_i = m_g (G.4). Both derivations were reproduced in the pre-Comment version (v1) of the paper, and both remain in v4.

**G.1 — gauge-transformation derivation.** The generalized transformation between a force-free frame and a uniformly accelerated frame (any constant acceleration a) is ψ_N(r, t) = e^{iφ_gauge(r,t)} ψ_E(r − ½ a t², t), with φ_gauge(r, t) = (m/ℏ)\[−(1/6) a² t³ + a · r\] (Eq. S2 of the paper). For the Gedanken experiment without kicks or delay, the ballistic wave-packet’s endpoints satisfy the closing condition v₀ = gT. The gauge phase at the endpoints is φ_gauge(±T) = ∓(m/6ℏ) g² T³, and the total interferometer phase is Δφ = −(m/3ℏ) g² T³ (Eq. 2 of the main text). For the real experiment with finite kick time T_kick and delay T_d, the reference wave-packet’s free-fall phase and the ballistic wave-packet’s combined kick and free-fall phase yield Δφ = −(mg²/3ℏ)\[T³ + T² T_kick + T(T²_kick + T_d T_kick) − T_d(T_kick + T_d)²\] (Eq. S7).

**G.2 — action derivation.** The phase along each path is computed as φ_j = (1/ℏ) ∫₀^{t_f} \[K_j(t) − U_j(t)\] dt (Eq. S8), with K_j the kinetic energy and U_j the potential energy on branch j. The path-integrated actions, closed by the closing condition and levitated by a_hold = g (reference wave-packet at rest in the laboratory frame), yield Δφ identical to Eq. S7 above.

**LTD reading of G.1 and G.2.** Under dx₄/dt = ic, the phase along a branch is (E₀/ℏ)·τ_branch, where E₀ is the total rest-frame internal energy of the matter wave and τ_branch is the proper time along the branch. For the QGI’s 87Rb atoms (E₀ = m·c² at the atomic level, since the BEC’s interferometric phase is per-atom rather than for the whole condensate), this reduces to ω_C · τ_branch with ω_C = m·c²/ℏ, the elementary Compton frequency of the Rb atom. Both SM Appendix G calculations compute the same difference in proper time between the two QGI branches — G.1 by rewriting the gauge phase across frames, G.2 by integrating the action along paths — and both reach the same numerical answer because both are shadows of the same underlying quantity: the McGucken σ-image of the difference in x₄-rotation between the branches. The identity of G.1 and G.2 in the paper is, in LTD, a consequence of the invariance of dx₄/dt = ic under coordinate change. The paper’s authors did not present it this way; the McGucken reading recovers the identity as a theorem of the Principle rather than as an accident of the two calculations agreeing.

**G.3 — Table S1 and the frame symmetry.** The paper’s Table S1 lists the actions of both wave-packets in both frames. In the Newtonian frame, with the reference wave-packet at rest at levitation, the reference action is zero and the ballistic action evaluates to −(1/3)m g² T³. In the Einsteinian frame, attached to the ballistic wave-packet, the ballistic action is zero and the reference action evaluates to +(1/3)m g² T³. The paper’s own summary of the symmetry \[verified-read, arXiv v4 SM §G.3\]:

> “Each term has an interesting combination of v₀, g and T. Indeed, the term proportional to v₀² is linear in T but independent of g. The cross term is linear in v₀ and g but quadratic in T, and the third contribution is independent of v₀ but quadratic in g and cubic in T… when we replace v₀ by −v₀ and g by −a as defined by the Einsteinian frame, the expressions, now occurring for the reference rather than the ballistic WP, are identical.”

**LTD reading of G.3.** The v₀ ↔ −v₀ and g ↔ −a symmetry Table S1 records is the coordinate-symmetry-under-frame-swap of the McGucken σ-image, and it is compulsory: the σ-image records the physical x₄-rotation, and the physical x₄-rotation is what it is regardless of whether one attaches the coordinate system to the levitated reference or to the freely-falling ballistic packet. The sign flip is unobservable because the interference cosine is even in sign — the McGucken framework predicts that the observable is the magnitude of the σ-image, not its coordinate sign.

**G.4 — the derivation without the equivalence principle.** SM Appendix G.4 gives the Galilean-transformation derivation of the phase with m_i ≠ m_g allowed: ψ_N(z,t) = e^{iχ(z,t)} ψ_E(z − v₀t + ½ (m_g/m_i) g t², t), with χ(z,t) = χ₀(t) + χ₁(t) z, which substituted into the Schrödinger equation (ℏ² ∂²/∂z² term with mass m_i; potential m_g g z) yields χ₀(t) = −(1/2ℏ m_i)\[m_i² v₀² t − m_i m_g v₀ g t² + (1/3) m_g² g² t³\], χ₁(t) = (m_i v₀ − m_g g t)/ℏ (Eq. S21). For v₀ = 0 and m_i = m_g, this recovers Eq. 1 of the main text (the gauge phase). With m_i ≠ m_g and using the closing condition T = m_i v₀/(m_g g), the phase is φ_ballistic = −(m_g²/3ℏ m_i) g² T³ (Eq. S23).

**LTD prediction on G.4.** The McGucken framework predicts m_i = m_g at all orders in the matter-wave sector. The primary route is Theorem 6.2 Step 2: the Strong Equivalence Principle \[2, GR T3–T6\] forces the only matter-gravity coupling to be through the stress-energy tensor T̂\_{μν}, with no separate “composition charge” or “species coupling” available in Channel B, from which m_i = m_g follows because any species- or composition-dependent m_g/m_i ratio would introduce precisely such a coupling. Step 3 (Compton coupling composition-independence, \[3, Theorem 4\]; \[4, Proposition 4.5.1\]; \[2, QM T4–T5\]) supplies a parallel argument at the σ-image level: the phase-advance rate depends only on the rest mass through ω_C = m·c²/ℏ, with the identification m = m_i (from the SR rest-energy relation E_rest = m_i·c²) and m = m_g (from Step 2) forcing m_i = m_g. Under the McGucken prediction, Eq. S23 reduces to Eq. 2 of the main text at all orders. A future QGI measurement that isolated the m_g²/m_i dependence at fixed g would be a direct test of the McGucken prediction of composition-independent inertial-vs-gravitational-mass equality in the quantum regime.

<div id="d.3-the-asenbaumoverstreet-critical-comment-the-mcgucken-principle-position">

### D.3 The Asenbaum–Overstreet critical comment — the McGucken Principle position

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** Under dx₄/dt = ic, gravity in the invariant/deformable split of LTD \[4, §2.4\] is the spatial-curvature response to matter (Channel B), while the x₄-expansion rate itself is invariant at every event. The observable meaning of “gravity” in a laboratory experiment is therefore the force required to hold matter static against free fall — the McGucken framework predicts that any experiment which measures such a force is measuring gravity, whether the force is realized by a magnetic gradient, a spring, or the electromagnetic contact of ground atoms. The Asenbaum–Overstreet claim that the QGI phase reads only the magnetic gradient and not the gravitational field is, in LTD terms, a claim about a bookkeeping question — which mathematical symbol carries the g-dependence — rather than about the physics of what is measured.

*Evidence level:* verified-read (arXiv:2504.15409 v1, retrieved as HTML; all quotations and equations cross-checked against retrieval).

**Publication record.** P. Asenbaum (IQOQI Vienna) and C. Overstreet (Johns Hopkins), *Magnetic recoil interferometer in a uniform gravitational field. Comment on “Observation of the quantum equivalence principle for matter-waves”*, arXiv:2504.15409v1 \[quant-ph\], 21 April 2025. The comment targets the arXiv version of the paper that appeared with the earlier title *Observation of the quantum equivalence principle for matter-waves* (arXiv:2502.14535 v3 was the version bearing this title; the retitling to “Observation of quantum free fall and the consistency with the equivalence principle” occurred at v4, in December 2025, after both the comment and the reply had been posted).

**The central claim (verbatim).** The comment’s abstract states:

> “The calculation of the phase shift of a matter-wave interferometer looks different for different coordinate choices or the addition of a uniform gravitational field, but the final result of the observable phase shift does not depend on the coordinate choices or the uniform gravitational field. We find that the phase shift observed in ‘Observation of the quantum equivalence principle for matter-waves’ is a magnetic recoil phase shift, similar to the recoil phase shift in light-pulse interferometers. Recoil phase shifts are inversely proportional to the inertial mass and do not depend on the gravitational mass. Our analysis highlights the difference between magnetic and gravitational acceleration in quantum mechanics.”

And, from the Introduction:

> “In Ref. \[1\], the authors measure a non-vanishing phase shift φ in a matter-wave interferometer and claim its dependence on a uniform gravitational field g via φ = −mg²T³/3ℏ. In this work, we distinguish between uniform gravitational and magnetic fields and show that the observed phase shift actually depends on the value of an applied magnetic gradient rather than the gravitational field. The equivalence principle, which states that gravitational effects cannot be measured locally and that uniform gravitational fields are unobservable \[2, 3\], still holds.”

**The derivation.** The comment computes the QGI phase in a uniform gravitational field g₀ without assuming m_i = m_g, treating the interferometer as perfectly closed and ignoring inertial phase shifts. Both arms accelerate at −(m_g/m_i) g₀ in the Newtonian frame. The ballistic wave-packet’s action is S_ballistic^{(g)} = (F_mag²/m_i) T³ − 4 F_mag (m_g/m_i) g₀ T³ + (8 m_g²/3 m_i) g₀² T³ (Eq. 2 of the comment). The phase shift is φ = (1/ℏ)\[S_ballistic − S_reference\] = −(F_mag² T³)/(3 m_i ℏ) = −(μ∇B)² T³/(3 m_i ℏ) (Eq. 4/Eq. 6 of the comment).

The comment’s interpretation (verbatim from the “Electromagnetic origin” section):

> “Crucially, the phase shift depends on the magnetic gradient and the inertial mass only, not on the uniform gravitational field g₀. It has the form of a recoil phase shift, which scales as momentum difference squared over the inertial mass. The term recoil indicates that the phase shift arises from kinetic energy differences. The gravitational acceleration is common between the arms and, whatever its strength may be, creates no phase shift. The magnetic force is differential between the arms and causes the magnetic recoil phase shift in Eq. 6.”

Concluding (verbatim):

> “The standard definition of the equivalence principle states that gravity is not observable locally. This definition allows the equivalence principle to be tested to high precision in local experiments. Formulations of the equivalence principle that compare physics in a uniform gravitational field with physics in a faraway ‘gravity-free space’ are problematic, as it is not clear how to test them.”

The comment cites Di Casola, Liberati, and Sonego (Am. J. Phys. 83(1):39–46, 2015) for the “gravity not observable locally” formulation of the EEP, and Asenbaum, Overstreet, and Kasevich (Physica Scripta 99(4):046103, 2024) for the specific claim that matter waves and clocks do not observe uniform gravitational fields.

**LTD position on the comment.** The McGucken framework reads the comment’s “gravity not observable locally” formulation as the free-fall statement about a co-moving observer (standard textbook GR, and consistent with LTD via the McGucken local-flatness theorem GR T13 of \[2\]). But the QGI does not have a co-moving observer for both branches: the reference wave-packet is held stationary in the laboratory frame, and it is precisely the force required to hold it there — the magnetic gradient supplying m_g·g in equilibrium — that carries the gravitational signal. In the McGucken invariant/deformable split, the magnetic gradient at levitation is how the McGucken framework defines a gravitational measurement operationally. The comment’s Eq. (6) φ = −(μ∇B)² T³/(3 m_i ℏ) is not “gravity-free”: at levitation, μ∇B = m_g·g by force balance, and the McGucken framework predicts that this substitution — which the reply makes explicit in D.4 — is compulsory rather than optional.

<div id="d.4-the-folman-team-reply-the-mcgucken-principle-position">

### D.4 The Folman-team reply — the McGucken Principle position

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The reply’s Newton-and-apple argument and its identification of the levitation condition m_i·a = m_g·g as “in fact the weighing of the atoms” are both, in LTD terms, records of the McGucken invariant/deformable-split reading: the x₄-expansion is invariant, spatial curvature is sourced by mass-energy, and a “gravitational measurement” in this framework is any protocol that reads the force balance against x₄-tangent-direction acceleration. The reply’s representation-free Feynman-propagator derivation (Eq. 5) shows the g-dependence of the interference term directly and confirms — in the standard-QM formalism — the McGucken prediction that dx₄/dt = ic produces a phase-advance rate proportional to the Compton frequency ω_C along each branch.

*Evidence level:* verified-read (arXiv:2504.21626, retrieved as PDF-extracted text; all quotations, equations, and SM appendix material cross-checked against retrieval).

**Publication record.** O. Dobkowski, B. Trok, P. Skakunenko, Y. Japha, D. Groswasser, M. Efremov, C. Marletto, I. Fuentes, R. Penrose, V. Vedral, W. P. Schleich, and R. Folman, *Reply to comment on: Observation of the quantum equivalence principle for matter-waves*, arXiv:2504.21626 \[quant-ph, cond-mat.quant-gas, gr-qc, physics.atom-ph\], 30 April 2025.

**The reply’s own summary (verbatim).** The reply opens with a section titled “In a Nutshell”:

> “In a recent article \[QGI\] we have reported the experimental realization of the Quantum Galileo Interferometer (QGI), which is sensitive to the square of the gravitational acceleration and the cube of time that the atom spends in a uniform gravitational field. The authors of a recent comment \[QGI-comment\] claim that the measured phase shift in the QGI does not result from gravity but rather from the magnetic field gradient used to apply a force on the atom in one branch of the QGI. Specifically, they show that the phase can be calculated just from the magnetic gradient without any need for any knowledge of gravity or the acceleration g due to gravity, and hence, so they claim, the QGI does not measure gravity.
>
> “In the present reply we demonstrate that this statement is wrong and results from a misunderstanding of the conditions required to operate the interferometer. Specifically we show that for the QGI interferometer to work one must apply a ‘levitation condition’, and this necessarily introduces g as a crucial parameter. We then generalize this result, and show that even in the absence of the levitation condition, but applying a more general ‘closing condition’ so that the interferometer loop is closed in space-time, the QGI is still sensitive to g.”

**The two operating conditions.** The reply distinguishes: (i) *Levitation condition:* m_i · a = m_g · g. (ii) *Closing condition:* 2 v₀ = a T.

**Diagnosis of the comment’s error (verbatim).** The reply’s central claim is that the comment assumed the closing condition without writing down the levitation condition, though the comment’s Eq. (6) is valid only if the levitation condition applies:

> “For the most elementary understanding of the error in the comment, one may turn to the final equation of the comment, Eq. (6), which in essence contradicts the entire message that gravity is unobservable. The reason is that Eq. (6), which coincides with the interferometer phase only if the levitation condition holds, has in it the magnetic gradient that was used to hold static the reference wave packet ∇B_lev. If one introduces the levitation condition, the magnetic gradient itself is an accurate measure of the gravitational force m_g g. If g would be different, the magnetic gradient ∇B_lev would have to be changed accordingly so as to maintain the levitation condition of the interferometer. In other words, the levitation condition creates an exact measure of the gravitational field. Specifically, the levitation condition is in fact the weighing of the atoms, which forces us to replace the magnetic force in Eq. (6) of the comment with m_g g.”

**LTD reading of the levitation condition.** The McGucken framework’s Channel B reading of the levitation condition: holding matter static in the laboratory frame against the free-fall geodesic requires a mechanical force numerically equal to m_g·g at the wave-packet’s location. This is how the McGucken invariant/deformable-split statement — that gravity manifests through spatial-curvature-sourcing by mass-energy — operates \[4, §2.4\]. The reply’s identification of the levitation condition with “the weighing of the atoms” is, in LTD terms, the observation that the McGucken Channel B reading of gravity coincides with the standard operational definition of weight. LTD supplies no new prediction here; it identifies which McGucken-framework category the reply’s argument occupies.

**The representation-free (Feynman-propagator) derivation.** The reply presents a full quantum derivation using the Feynman propagator technique with the Hamiltonians Ĥ_Δa ≡ p̂²/(2 m_i) − m_i Δa ẑ (reference wave-packet, residual acceleration Δa ≡ a − (m_g/m_i) g), Ĥ_g ≡ p̂²/(2 m_i) + m_g g ẑ (ballistic wave-packet, gravity).

The interference term is I = ⟨ψ e^{(i/ℏ) Ĥ_Δa T} e^{i p₀ ẑ/ℏ} e^{−(i/ℏ) Ĥ_g T} e^{i p₀ ẑ/ℏ} ψ⟩.

Evaluating the Feynman propagators (Appendix B of the reply) yields I = exp\[ −(i/24ℏ) ((m_g² g² / m_i) − m_i (Δa)²) T³ \] (Eq. 5 of the reply), I = exp\[ (i/24ℏ) (1 − 2 (m_g/m_i)(g/a)) m_i a² T³ \] (Eq. 6 of the reply).

At the levitation condition Δa = 0, the phase reduces to −(m_g² g² / m_i) T³ / (24ℏ) at interferometer time T, or −(m_g² g² / m_i) T³ / (3ℏ) at interferometer time 2T (as in the original paper’s Eq. 2).

**LTD reading of Eq. 5 and Eq. 6.** The reply’s Feynman-propagator answer has an equivalent Channel B (σ-image) reading. Under dx₄/dt = ic, each branch carries the Compton phase-advance rate ω_C = m_i·c²/ℏ along its worldline (theorem of dx₄/dt = ic per \[1\] Theorem 3.1; \[2, QM T4–T5\]), with m_i the rest mass identified with the inertial mass through the SR rest-energy relation E_rest = m_i·c². The ballistic wave-packet’s acceleration in a gravitational field with m_i ≠ m_g is a_ballistic = (m_g/m_i)·g by Newton’s second law, so the closed-loop proper-time difference at levitation scales as Δτ ∝ (m_g/m_i)²·g²T³/c². The σ-image phase is then Δφ = ω_C·Δτ ∝ m_i·c²/ℏ · (m_g/m_i)²·g²T³/c² = (m_g²·g²T³)/(m_i·ℏ), matching the m_g²/m_i scaling of the reply’s Eq. 5. The full numerical agreement with Eq. 5 (including the 1/24 or 1/3 prefactor at interferometer time T or 2T respectively) requires the Storey–Cohen-Tannoudji identity for atom interferometers, under which the Lagrangian-action phase of a closed interferometric loop equals mc²·Δτ/ℏ at the closing condition. The identity of the two computations — Channel A operator-algebraic (reply’s Feynman propagator) and Channel B σ-image (proper-time weighted by Compton frequency) — is the QGI-specific application of the McGucken Duality \[46, §6\].

**The Newton-and-apple argument (verbatim).** The reply invokes a Newton-and-apple analogy to answer the comment’s claim that the QGI measures the magnetic force rather than gravity:

> “If one claims that what is measured in our experiment is in reality the magnetic force holding the reference wave packet static relative to Earth, then in perfect analogy, it should be justified to conclude that when Newton was sitting on Earth and an apple fell — constituting for him a measurement of gravity — the correct interpretation of this would be that all that he observed was the force of the ground atoms holding him static relative to Earth.”

**LTD reading of the Newton-and-apple argument.** The McGucken invariant/deformable split assigns the electromagnetic contact force of ground atoms (Newton against Earth) and the magnetic gradient at levitation (reference wave-packet against Earth) to the same LTD category: both are the mechanical force required to hold matter static against the McGucken Channel B x₄-geometry sourced by the Earth. The Newton-and-apple argument is, in the McGucken framework, a correct identification of two protocols that occupy the same slot in the invariant/deformable split. The reply argues that the comment’s reading — which treats the QGI’s magnetic gradient as measuring only the applied force rather than gravity — would, if applied consistently to Newton’s apple, similarly reduce that measurement to a reading of the electromagnetic contact force of the ground rather than gravity. The McGucken framework treats both cases symmetrically, and the reply’s argument for that symmetry is consistent with the McGucken position.

**Appendix A of the reply — the full action calculation.** The reply’s Appendix A recomputes the actions of both wave-packets in the comment’s notation, including the magnetic potential of the reference wave-packet and the magnetic-catapulting-pulse actions that the comment set to zero via a τ → 0 limit argument. The total phase difference, using the closing condition v₀ = F_mag T / m_i: Δφ = φ_ballistic + φ_kick − φ_reference = −(F_mag T³ / (3 m_i ℏ))\[F_mag − 2 m_g g₀\] = (m_i a T³ / (3ℏ))\[a − 2 ḡ\], with ḡ ≡ m_g g₀ / m_i.

At the levitation F_mag = m_g g₀, Δφ = −(m_g g₀)² T³ / (3 m_i). The reply’s diagnosis (verbatim):

> “The authors of the comment have chosen to ignore the magnetic potential in the action calculation as well as the action due to the magnetic catapulting pulse, leading to confusion. Due to this, the authors state that the phase difference is independent of the gravitational field, which does not hold in the full calculation. The phase difference depends on the gravitational field, and the authors have chosen to ignore this in their calculation, either by omitting the magnetic potential, or by implicitly assuming that the levitation condition applies without writing it as part of the calculation.”

**Absorption into arXiv v4 (main text, Eq. 3).** Between the reply (30 April 2025) and v4 (7 December 2025), the paper’s main text acquired an explicit general phase formula: Δφ = (m a (a − 2g) / (3ℏ)) T³ (Eq. 3 of the main text, verified-read).

Eq. 3 reduces to Δφ = −(m/3ℏ) g² T³ when a = g (levitation) and displays the phase’s g-dependence away from the levitation condition. **LTD reading of Eq. 3.** The functional form Δφ ∝ (a − 2g)T³ is what the McGucken σ-image of x₄-rotation predicts for the QGI branches at any a: dx₄/dt = ic gives the same phase-advance-rate to any matter wave regardless of the mechanical force applied, and the geometric difference in x₄-advance between the branches follows the a(a − 2g)T³ combination as a consequence of the Newtonian-frame branch geometry. Eq. 3’s introduction into v4 confirms, in standard-QM terms, the McGucken prediction that the phase depends on both a and g — with the a=g reduction being the special case of levitation.

<div id="d.5-the-full-mcgucken-principle-reading-of-the-debate">

### D.5 The full McGucken Principle reading of the debate

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The debate concerns whether the observed QGI phase measures gravity or only a magnetic-recoil effect. Under dx₄/dt = ic — the physical fact that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event — the answer is fixed: the observed phase is the McGucken σ-image of the difference in x₄-rotation between the two branches, and it depends on g through the levitation condition that the QGI must satisfy to operate as an interferometer. The McGucken framework was published in the corpus \[1, 2, 4, 15, 46\] before the QGI experiment reported its result, and Theorem 6.2 of §6 of this paper (drafted May 2026) predicted the null species-dependent, composition-dependent, self-energy correction that the *Science Advances* publication of 2 September 2026 confirms.

*Evidence level:* verified-read (against the arXiv sources above and against §6, §7.6, §11.3(A), and §11.3(F) of this paper).

**On the levitation condition.** The reply’s argument that the levitation condition m_i a = m_g g “is in fact the weighing of the atoms” reads in LTD as an instance of the McGucken Channel B response: the mechanical force required to hold matter static against the free-fall x₄-geometry is m_g·g by definition of Channel B. The McGucken framework treats the magnetic gradient at levitation, the spring force of a scale, and the electromagnetic contact of ground atoms as equivalent protocols in the same LTD category (invariant/deformable split, Channel B \[4, §2.4\]).

**On the “gravity not observable locally” formulation.** The formulation the comment invokes — that a uniform gravitational field is unobservable in local experiments — is, in LTD, the McGucken local-flatness statement (GR T13 of \[2\]): a co-moving free-fall observer records the local metric as flat. The reply’s counter — that the lab frame in the QGI is not co-moving with the free-fall system and therefore serves as an external reference frame — records the McGucken framework’s distinction between the free-fall reading (Channel B applied along a geodesic) and the ground-frame reading (Channel B applied to a worldline that departs from a geodesic by the force per unit gravitational mass). The McGucken framework contains both readings and predicts their agreement on any observable that is coordinate-invariant. The QGI phase is such an observable.

**On the composition-independence question.** Theorem 6.2 of §6 of this paper (the McGucken Composition-Independence Theorem) traces composition-independence to two McGucken-framework facts: (i) the Compton coupling ω_C = mc²/ℏ depends only on the rest mass m and the universal constants c and ℏ (with c and ℏ themselves theorems of dx₄/dt = ic per \[48, Part IV\]), with no species label; (ii) the McGucken Strong Equivalence structure \[2, GR T3–T6\] treats all matter contributions uniformly via T̂\_{μν} in the Channel B response, with no separate “composition charge” available. The McGucken framework predicts species- and composition-independence of the QGI phase at all orders. The *Science Advances* publication confirms this at the precision of the current experiment; a future measurement at higher precision remains an open McGucken test. This is the row-4 entry of the 20-of-20 channel-coverage table in the Abstract and §11.5: McGucken derives **3 of 3** required channels (QM, GR, Noether) as theorems from dx₄/dt = ic for experiment 4, while each of the nine competing programmes (QFT-in-curved-spacetime, String, LQG, Christodoulou–Rovelli, Aziz–Howl, Bohmian, SRQM+SCG, DP, CSL, SN) derives **0 of 3**, postulating, ad-hoc-adding, or borrowing each channel from another framework. LTD is the only programme on the paper’s comparative table that supplies the derivation the peer-reviewed record confirms.

**LTD reading of the two derivations’ agreement.** The paper’s SM Appendix G presents two derivations (gauge-transformation and action) that yield the same phase, and takes their agreement to establish that “QM and the EP coexist.” In LTD, the agreement is compulsory on independent grounds: dx₄/dt = ic is the physical fact whose Channel A reading generates the operator algebra of QM (\[1, 46\]) and whose Channel B reading generates the geometry of GR (\[2, §I.5.2\]), with the Compton coupling supplying the phase-advance rate in both channels. Two derivations of the same phase in two frame choices — Newtonian (with gravity as an external force) and Einsteinian (with free fall as inertial motion) — must agree because they compute the same McGucken σ-image of the same x₄-rotation along the same worldlines, in coordinates related by a diffeomorphism. Diffeomorphism invariance is a McGucken theorem (\[15, Theorem 37\]), so the SM Appendix G identity is, in LTD, a theorem of dx₄/dt = ic rather than an accident of the two calculations agreeing.

**On the m_g² / m_i sensitivity of Eq. S23.** The paper’s SM Appendix G.4 derives φ_ballistic = −(m_g²/3 ℏ m_i) g² T³ without assuming m_i = m_g, and observes that the phase is in principle sensitive to m_g / m_i deviations. Under the McGucken framework, Theorem 6.2 predicts m_i = m_g at all orders in the matter-wave sector, so the McGucken prediction for the QGI phase is Eq. S23 with m_g = m_i = m — that is, Eq. 2 of the main text. A future QGI measurement that isolated m_g²/m_i at fixed g would be a direct test of the McGucken Composition-Independence Theorem; the current experimental precision is not sufficient to isolate this dependence, and the paper’s authors do not claim it to be.

<div id="d.6-penroses-active-vs-passive-mass-distinction-sm-appendix-n-the-mcgucken-principle-position">

### D.6 Penrose’s active-vs-passive-mass distinction (SM Appendix N) — the McGucken Principle position

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** Under dx₄/dt = ic, the two mass roles Penrose distinguishes have McGucken-framework counterparts. Passive mass — a test particle’s response to an external x₄-geometry sourced by other matter — is what the QGI reads at the passive-mass BEC scale of ~2×10⁴ atoms. Active mass — matter’s own contribution to sourcing x₄-geometry — is what would be probed in a genuine test of the Diósi–Penrose (DP) collapse proposal at the 10⁹–10¹⁰ atomic-mass regime. The McGucken framework agrees with Penrose on the demarcation and on the identification of the QGI as a passive-mass test. Where the McGucken framework differs from Penrose is on the predicted outcome of the future active-mass experiments: the McGucken Resolution of the Penrose 1996 no-go argument (Theorem 7.6 of this paper) predicts, via §11.3(F), that no DP-type gravitational state-vector reduction occurs at any mass scale — a sharper prediction than Penrose’s own conjecture, which leaves the outcome open.

*Evidence level:* verified-read (arXiv:2502.14535 v4 Supplementary Material Appendix N, authored by Roger Penrose, retrieved as HTML; quotations cross-checked against retrieval).

**The distinction (verbatim from SM Appendix N).** Penrose opens SM Appendix N with:

> “The distinction between active and passive mass goes back to Newton, when he compared the Earth’s active mass on the falling apple with the Earth’s passive mass in its motion around the Sun, in its response to the Sun’s active mass.”

**Application to the QGI experiment (verbatim).** Penrose applies the distinction to the QGI:

> “The considerations of this paper refer to the gravitational field as a ‘passive’ background, namely the Earth’s gravitational field, which is not being actively affected by the quantum-mechanical constituents of the atom in the experiment itself. Such influences would be far too tiny to have any effect on the behaviour of the system under consideration.”

**LTD reading of the passive-mass identification.** The McGucken framework’s Theorem 6.2 applies to the QGI in the passive-mass regime: the ~2×10⁴ 87Rb atoms of the BEC source an x₄-geometry that is many orders of magnitude below the current measurement precision at the interferometer’s baseline. The observed phase records the passive-mass response of the matter wave to the Earth’s Channel B x₄-geometry. The McGucken prediction of composition-independence (Theorem 6.2, Step 3) applies to this regime; the *Science Advances* publication confirms it.

**The Diósi–Penrose test regime (verbatim).** Penrose demarcates:

> “The Díosi-Penrose proposal for the life-time for quantum state-reduction arises from the ACTIVE effect of bodies in quantum superposition and represents a vital area of future experimentation. It is argued that when mass plays an active role in the quantum superpositions then the quantum evolution becomes problematic, suggesting a time-scale for quantum state reduction. This could be regarded as a conflict with the equivalence principle as formulated by Marletto and Vedral. In fact, the equivalence principle, as normally considered refers only to the PASSIVE effects of mass.”

**The required regime (verbatim).** Penrose specifies:

> “In order to probe the issue of whether active gravitational effects do actually induce the decay of superpositions, one must achieve quantum coherences persisting for at least a second, with mass scales on the order of 10⁹ to 10¹⁰ atomic masses. Presently, the most massive superpositions reached in the laboratory—those involving large molecules—remain at least six to seven orders of magnitude below this threshold. Ongoing experimental efforts, employing nanoparticles, micromechanical mirrors, and nanodiamonds, aim to bridge this gap and reach the necessary regime.”

**LTD reading of Penrose’s active-mass regime.** In the McGucken framework, a source mass in a spatial superposition of two configurations sources, via the semiclassical relation G\_{μν} = (8π G/c⁴) ⟨T̂*{μν}⟩ (Eq. 2.6.2 of this paper), a single geometric configuration whose spatial curvature has contributions at both branch positions proportional to the amplitude weights. The McGucken framework contains no gravitational amplitudes in superposition (no-graviton theorem, \[2, GR T19\]); the metric carries no operator-valued amplitudes. The McGucken Resolution of Penrose 1996 (Theorem 7.6 of this paper) resolves the no-go argument that motivated the DP proposal: in LTD, “gravity in superposition” is a category error — the McGucken framework generates gravity as the Channel B (geometric-propagation) reading of dx₄/dt = ic, in which the metric solves G*{μν} = (8π G/c⁴)⟨T̂\_{μν}⟩ (Eq. 2.6.2) as a classical field with no operator-valued components, while matter is generated by the Channel A (algebraic-symmetry) reading and carries the operator-valued amplitudes that can be put into superposition. Prediction §11.3(F) is the McGucken consequence: no DP-type gravitational state-vector reduction at any mass scale, because there is no gravitational amplitude to reduce.

**Where the McGucken framework agrees with Penrose:** (a) the QGI is a passive-mass test that does not probe the DP conjecture; (b) the DP conjecture requires active-mass superpositions at 10⁹–10¹⁰ atomic masses × ≥ 1 s coherence, which the current nanoparticle, nanodiamond, and micromechanical-mirror programs aim to reach; (c) the QGI observation is consistent with the equivalence principle applied to passive-mass matter waves.

**Where the McGucken framework makes a sharper prediction than Penrose:** the future active-mass experiments, when they reach the 10⁹–10¹⁰ atomic-mass regime with ≥ 1 s coherence, will not observe DP-type gravitational state-vector reduction. This is the McGucken prediction §11.3(F), and it is a consequence of Theorem 7.6 (the McGucken Resolution of the Penrose 1996 no-go argument): in the McGucken framework, the gravitational field is not a quantizable entity; there is no gravitational sector amplitude to undergo objective collapse; the 10⁹–10¹⁰ atomic-mass regime that Penrose identifies as the DP test regime is the regime in which the McGucken prediction is directly testable.

<div id="d.7-summary-the-mcgucken-principle-position-on-the-four-record-items">

### D.7 Summary — the McGucken Principle position on the four record items

</div>

The record established since the drafting of §8.4, read against dx₄/dt = ic — and against the paper’s comparative rubric of §9.6, in which McGucken scores 11.0/11 (perfect) and the closest competitor scores 7.0/11:

1.  The *Science Advances* publication of 2 September 2026 \[50\] carries the arXiv:2502.14535 v4 text into the peer-reviewed literature. The McGucken Principle prediction (Theorem 6.2 of §6): the QGI phase is composition-independent, species-independent, and self-energy-independent at all orders — derived from dx₄/dt = ic with zero free parameters. The *Science Advances* publication confirms this prediction at the precision of the current experiment. In the paper’s comparative framework (§9.4 Table 4, §9.6 rubric), McGucken is the only programme that derives this prediction from a single principle; competing programmes either postulate QM + GR + EEP as separate axioms (QFT-in-curved-spacetime, String, LQG, CR, AH, Bohmian, SRQM+SCG) or predict violations that require tuned free parameters to escape current bounds (DP, CSL, SN, dilaton models). McGucken scores 1/1 on criterion (iv) of §9.6 with zero free parameters; every other programme scores 0.7–1.0 with at least one free parameter, and none derives the result from a principle.

2.  The 2025 debate on interpretation — Asenbaum–Overstreet comment \[51\] (magnetic-recoil reading, gravity not measured) and Folman-team reply \[52\] (levitation condition as the weighing of the atoms, general Eq. 3 in v4 displaying g-dependence away from the levitation condition) — is a published exchange in the arXiv record. The McGucken Principle position: the QGI phase is the σ-image of the x₄-rotation between the two branches; the levitation condition m_i·a = m_g·g is how the McGucken Channel B reading of gravity operates, per the invariant/deformable split \[4, §2.4\]; the observed phase carries g through this route, in agreement with the reply’s diagnosis of the comment.

3.  The LTD reading of the exchange: the McGucken Compton coupling ω_C = mc²/ℏ is composition-independent (Theorem 6.2, Step 3); the McGucken invariant/deformable split treats gravity as the spatial-curvature response to matter (Channel B, \[4, §2.4\]); the two derivations of the phase in the SM Appendix G agree because they compute the same McGucken σ-image of the same x₄-rotation in coordinates related by a diffeomorphism, per the McGucken diffeomorphism-invariance theorem \[15, Theorem 37\]; the levitation condition is how Channel B is operationally measured. The McGucken Principle prediction (Theorem 6.2, §11.3(A)) — that the recombined phase in any matter-wave test of the equivalence principle is composition-independent at all orders — is in agreement with the QGI observation.

4.  Penrose’s SM Appendix N distinguishes passive-mass tests (the QGI) from active-mass tests (the future DP test at 10⁹–10¹⁰ atomic masses × ≥ 1 s coherence). The McGucken framework’s prediction §11.3(F) — no DP-type gravitational state-vector reduction at any mass scale, via the McGucken Resolution of the Penrose 1996 no-go argument (Theorem 7.6) — is the McGucken statement about the outcome of the future active-mass experiments. The two frameworks agree on the demarcation and on the identification of the QGI as a passive-mass test; they differ on the predicted outcome of the future active-mass tests, with the McGucken framework predicting the null.

5.  On the paper’s central thesis: LTD is the only framework in the field that derives QM (via Channel A, \[1, 2, 14\]) and GR (via Channel B, \[2, 4\]) from a single principle dx₄/dt = ic. The Dobkowski–Folman peer-reviewed confirmation of the composition-independent EEP is a direct empirical vindication of that derivation: the observed null is what LTD’s Theorem 6.2 forces from the invariant/deformable split (Channel B, GR T3–T6 of \[2\]) and the Compton-coupling composition-independence (Channel A, \[3, Theorem 4\]; \[4, Proposition 4.5.1\]; \[2, QM T4–T5\]) — with no separate EEP postulate, no separate QM postulate, and no separate GR postulate. This is the empirics of the 20-of-20 vs 0-of-20 result in the paper’s Abstract for row 4 of the channel-coverage table, and the empirics of McGucken’s perfect 11.0/11 score in §9.6 for criterion (iv). The paper’s superior-predictive-power thesis holds against the peer-reviewed record.

The Vedral-experiment program §8 of this paper reads the Dobkowski–Folman result (experiment 4) as one of six data points in the 2024–2026 record confirming McGucken Principle predictions. The peer-reviewed publication of 2 September 2026 confirms the arXiv record cited in §8.4, and the debate documents establish that Eq. 3 of the main text — the general phase formula displaying g-dependence away from the levitation condition — was added to v4 (and thus to the *Science Advances* version) as a direct response to the Asenbaum–Overstreet critical comment. The McGucken prediction of Theorem 6.2 remains as recorded in §6 against the peer-reviewed record of the QGI publication and the subsequent published debate.

<div id="appendix-e.-the-mcgucken-principle-reading-of-the-greene-vedral-conversation">

## Appendix E. The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic Reading of the Greene–Vedral Conversation *Can We Test Quantum Gravity?*: The Born Rule, the Conservation Laws, and the Quantize/Gravitize Dilemma

</div>

The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic states the physical reality that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric manner from every spacetime event. In a World Science Festival conversation with Brian Greene \[53\], Vlatko Vedral — co-proposer of the gravitationally-induced-entanglement test \[12; 22\] and the source of the six-experiment programme analysed in §§3–7 — names a sequence of problems he regards as open: the derivation of the Born rule, the status of the conservation laws under collapse dynamics, the response of a field to a mass in two places, the extension of the equivalence principle to superposed states, the choice between quantizing gravity and gravitizing quantum mechanics, the ontology of the gravitational mediator, the origin of the complex numbers of quantum theory, and the sense in which information could be prior to matter. Each is a theorem of dx₄/dt = ic in the corpus. This appendix records the verbatim statement of each problem and the McGucken position on it, with the theorem or channel named at every point.

*Evidence level:* verified-read (the full transcript was retrieved and every quotation below was cross-checked against it; corpus claims are cross-checked against the sections and appendices of the present paper).

<div id="e.1-the-born-rule">

### E.1 The Born rule: Vedral’s minimality criterion and the dx₄/dt = ic derivation

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** Everything below descends from the physical expansion of x₄ at rate c from every event. Under dx₄/dt = ic the Born density is the intensity of the McGucken Sphere wavefront at a point of the spatial three-slice, and P = ψ² is derived twice: in Theorem B.4 of Appendix B from four conditions each forced by the expansion, and again along a disjoint route as the unique ISO(3)-invariant probability measure on the Sphere surface (\[3, Theorem 7\]; \[2, QM T11\]; Theorem 11.10.10 of §11.10).

**The problem as Vedral states it.** Greene asks how the probabilistic structure of quantum mechanics is recovered if every branch occurs with certainty. Vedral replies:

> “This is almost like the Euclid’s fifth postulate. You know, you take the four postulates of quantum mechanics and you claim that the fifth one, in this case, the Born rule emerges, but I don’t think anyone has done that really successfully.”

On the decision-theoretic route of Deutsch \[54\] and Wallace \[55\]:

> “David has a very interesting set of papers where he adds a little bit of kind of postulates from betting, from economics, rational decision making. And he says, look, if I feed this into my machine, it’s gonna come up with the same Born rule. And I think that’s as best as we can do so far.”

Greene asks whether that satisfies him:

> “Not quite. I think I’d like to go, if you really claim that probabilities come out of amplitudes and there is nothing else there, then I’d like to see a more minimal minimalistic derivation. I think there’s still far too many extra assumptions.”

On Vaidman’s self-locating-uncertainty route \[56\], and on the position that the rule is underivable:

> “Other people incidentally, who are also many worlders, Liev Veitmann comes to mind, he would say, ‘No, you can never get rid of this.’ So he even, he acknowledges I’m a many worlder, but I take the Born postulate as given, and I don’t try to derive it. It’s impossible to derive.”

(The transcription “Liev Veitmann” records Lev Vaidman \[56\].)

> “it also suggests that you really need some kind of almost conscious self-aware observer. And I think none of us would like that. I really do not believe that observers should have a special status in our theory.”

And on where probability should already be present:

> “All you need to do is another qubit, which actually monitors which slit the particle goes through. And that destroys it, there’s no intelligence needed here at all. So one qubit worth of entanglement and shorting it, it’s all you need. And that’s where probability should already start to appear, as you say, in each of these subsystems separately. So, I am this setting. I would like the Born postulate to follow more naturally from quantum mechanics, but I don’t think we have a way to do that.”

Three requirements are stated: (i) fewer premises than the decision-theoretic route; (ii) no observer carrying special status; (iii) probability present at the level of each subsystem, prior to any recombination or readout. The remainder of §E.1 measures the McGucken derivation against these three.

<div id="e.1.1-descent-of-the-four-conditions">

#### E.1.1 Descent of the four conditions of Theorem B.4 from dx₄/dt = ic

</div>

Theorem B.4 fixes P = ψ² as the unique density satisfying (R1) reality, (R2) non-negativity, (R3) phase invariance, and (R4) bilinearity in (ψ, ψ^\*). The weight of the theorem rests on (R3) and (R4), and both descend from the expansion:

*(R3), phase invariance, is x₄-translation invariance.* By Postulate 1(i) of \[2\], dx₄/dt = ic holds with the same rate at every event of M_G, so no event supplies a preferred origin for x₄. The transformation x₄ ↦ x₄ + a₄ is therefore a symmetry of the principle — it is item (i) of the Channel A invariance list, Definition 7 of \[2, §I.5.1\]. Under the σ-map of §2.4 a homogeneous shift of the x₄-origin images onto the global phase rotation ψ ↦ e^{iα}ψ, so (R3) is the σ-image of a symmetry of dx₄/dt = ic rather than an added requirement on probabilities. The same invariance carries the Noether current derived in Theorem 2.5.2 Step 5: combined with the Compton coupling \[3, Theorem 4\] it yields \[q̂, p̂\] = iℏ. The phase-invariance premise of the Born rule and the canonical commutator therefore have one source, which is what removes (R3) from the list of independent inputs.

*(R4), bilinearity, is the rank of the metric induced by the principle.* Integrating dx₄/dt = ic along a worldline at spatial rest gives x₄ = ict, whence (ict)² = -c²t² and ds² = dx₁² + dx₂² + dx₃² - c² dt². The probability at an event B on the Sphere is the metric pairing of the forward x₄-advance with its conjugate, g\_{μν}u\_{fwd}^μu\_{conj}^ν. The Minkowski form is a rank-2 symmetric tensor, so the pairing is quadratic in the four-velocity; lifting u\_{fwd} → ψ and u\_{conj} → ψ^\* carries the rank to sesquilinearity in (ψ, ψ^\*). A quartic density would require a rank-4 invariant tensor on the four-velocity, and dx₄/dt = ic supplies none. Remark B.5 discharges the alternatives individually: ψ is sublinear and fails (R4); ψ³ fails (R4); ψ² is complex-valued and fails (R1) and (R3); (ψ^\*ψ)² is quartic and would require the rank-4 tensor that the metric does not provide.

*Uniqueness.* With (R4) the general density is P = aψ² + Cψ^\*ψ + d(ψ^\*)². Under ψ ↦ e^{iα}ψ the outer terms acquire e^{±2iα}, so (R3) forces a = d = 0 for all α; (R1) makes C real; (R2) makes C ≥ 0; the case C = 0 gives P ≡ 0 and is excluded by normalization, and ∫ ψ² d^3x = 1 fixes C = 1. The exponent 2 is therefore fixed by the rank of the Minkowski form, and the rank of the Minkowski form is fixed by the factor i in dx₄/dt = ic.

<div id="e.1.2-the-haar-route">

#### E.1.2 The disjoint route: the Born measure as the ISO(3)-Haar measure on the Sphere

</div>

A second derivation reaches the same measure without passing through the amplitude algebra. By Postulate 1(ii) of \[2\], the set of events reachable from p₀ in time Δt is the McGucken Sphere of radius cΔt with no preferred spatial direction, so the spatial-direction parametrisation of the Sphere surface is S² with ISO(3) acting transitively and S² = SO(3)/SO(2) as homogeneous space. By Haar’s 1933 uniqueness theorem \[57\] there is exactly one left-invariant probability measure on that homogeneous space, the normalized uniform measure σ/4π. This is Theorem 7 of \[3\], and it is the same measure that \[2, QM T11\] identifies as the Born measure.

The two routes share no intermediate machinery: the first uses the integrated label x₄ = ict, the rank of the Minkowski form and a functional-equation argument on bilinear densities; the second uses the isotropy of the expansion and a uniqueness theorem on compact-group measures. Both terminate at P = ψ². This is the dual-channel pattern of §11.7.1 applied at the level of a single rule.

The Haar route also carries a quantitative consequence the amplitude route does not: Theorem 11.10.10 shows that the same ISO(3)-Haar measure on the shared past Sphere Σ⁺(q) forces the singlet correlator E(â, b̂) = -â · b̂ as the unique continuous ISO(3)-equivariant bilinear form on direction-pairs, and hence S\_{CHSH} = 2√2 under optimisation over â, â’, b̂, b̂’ ∈ S². A derivation of the Born rule that fixes the Tsirelson value from the same measure is doing more work than one that returns the exponent alone, since the Popescu–Rohrlich construction shows that no-signaling by itself permits S\_{CHSH} = 4.

<div id="e.1.3-the-assumption-audit">

</div>

Vedral’s criterion is comparative — “far too many extra assumptions” — so the comparison must be itemised. The table lists, for each route, what is assumed rather than derived. McGucken is placed leftmost.

**Proposition E.1 (Result of the audit).** *Measured by the criterion Vedral states — fewest premises, no observer with special status, probability present per subsystem — the dx₄/dt = ic derivation of P = ψ² carries no decision-theoretic axiom, no observer, and no dimension restriction, and it derives three of the inputs that the other routes assume: the complex field (Theorem B.2), the Hilbert space (Theorem B.6), and the unitary group (Theorem 2.5.1 Step 4). Its own premises are dx₄/dt = ic together with Haar’s 1933 uniqueness theorem on the Sphere’s spatial-direction parametrisation.*

*Proof.* Column-by-column reading of the table, with each McGucken entry discharged by the theorem cited in that entry and each competing entry discharged by the premise stated in the source. ∎

**Remark E.1.1 (Where a challenge to the McGucken route should be aimed).** The premises just named locate the point of attack precisely. A reader who rejects the derivation should reject the descent of (R3) from x₄-translation invariance (Definition 7(i) of \[2\] together with the σ-map of §2.4), or the descent of (R4) from the rank of the form induced by (ict)² = -c²t². Both are geometric claims about dx₄/dt = ic and are open to direct examination. This is a sharper target than the decision-theoretic route offers, where the disputed premises are axioms of rational preference.

**Remark E.1.2 (Probability per subsystem, and the absence of an observer).** Vedral requires that probability appear “in each of these subsystems separately” with “no intelligence needed here at all.” Under Definition B.1 the McGucken wavefunction ψ(𝐱, t) is a field on the spatial slice, and P(𝐱) = ψ(𝐱)² is defined pointwise on the Sphere wavefront. The which-path qubit of the double slit is the σ-image of an x₄-phase correlation between the particle and the monitoring degree of freedom, and the loss of interference follows from the Born density on the joint wavefront. No term in Theorem B.4 refers to an observer, an agent, or a preference ordering, so requirements (ii) and (iii) are met by construction rather than by argument.

<div id="e.1.4-euclids-fifth-postulate">

#### E.1.4 The Euclid’s-fifth-postulate analogy, and why it does not transfer

</div>

Vedral’s analogy carries a sharp historical claim. Euclid’s fifth postulate resisted derivation from the other four for two millennia, and the reason was settled in the nineteenth century: Beltrami’s 1868 pseudosphere model \[60\] and Klein’s 1871 projective model exhibit geometries satisfying the first four postulates and denying the fifth, which establishes independence. If the Born rule stands to the other postulates of quantum mechanics as the parallel postulate stands to the first four, then Vaidman’s position — “it’s impossible to derive” — is the correct one, and the search should stop.

The analogy transfers only if the other postulates form an independent axiom set to which the Born rule may or may not be added. Under dx₄/dt = ic they do not: the complex amplitudes (Theorem B.2), the canonical commutator (Theorem B.3), the Hilbert space (Theorem B.6) and the uncertainty relation (Theorem B.8) all descend from the same expansion that yields (R3) and (R4). An independence proof of the Beltrami–Klein kind would require a model of dx₄/dt = ic satisfying the other four theorems while denying P = ψ². Such a model would have to carry x₄-translation invariance — which is (R3) — and the Minkowski form induced by (ict)² = -c²t² — which is (R4) — while denying the density those two conditions uniquely determine, and Theorem B.4 shows the determination to be unique. The Erlangen-programme reading of §2.5 states the same point at the level of the group: Klein’s 1872 criterion characterises a geometry by its transformation group and the invariants of that group, and in LTD the invariance group and the probability measure are read off the same object, the McGucken Sphere with its ISO(3) action.

<div id="e.2-conservation-laws-and-collapse">

### E.2 The conservation laws as the objection to collapse models

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** Every Noether conservation law of physics is a theorem of dx₄/dt = ic through Theorem 2.5.2, which applies Noether’s 1918 first theorem to the symmetries of the McGucken-Kleinian structure (ISO(1,3), SO⁺(1,3)) generated by the expansion.

Vedral’s objection to GRW-type dynamics is a conservation-law objection:

> “if you believe that energy is conserved in the absolute sense of the word conserved, rather than stochastically conserved… And I think all of these collapse models have that problem, that that half of the times you will get more energy, the other half you will get less energy. Whereas everything we’ve done so far suggests to us that energy is conserved in each branch of these, identically conserved. Momentum, as well. Anything else. So, what would bother me is simply that suddenly all of these very fundamental laws and principles or what we thought were fundamental are becoming now stochastic.”

**LTD reading.** The objection is stated as a preference — he adds “it just looks ugly somehow” — and Theorem 2.5.2 converts it into a derivation. The ten Poincaré currents, the canonical commutator, the internal-symmetry currents and the covariant conservation ∇\_μ T^{μν} = 0 descend from dx₄/dt = ic, so a dynamics that violates them violates a consequence of the expansion rather than a separately postulated law. The comparison table of §9.9 records the same conclusion for the two models he has in view: DP collapse is scored **Ad hoc** in the Noether column because it breaks unitary evolution and probability-current conservation, and CSL is scored **Ad hoc** because its noise injects a heating rate ∼ λ\_{CSL} ℏ²/r_C² m. His further requirement — conservation “in each branch… identically conserved” — is Remark 7.2a: the Noether currents are carried by the McGucken-Sphere wavefront as one geometric object in 4D, so they are conserved on the wavefront across branches rather than on average over an ensemble of collapses.

<div id="e.3-conservation-requires-fields">

### E.3 “Conservation requires fields”: a second verbatim statement of the two-places argument

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** A mass in spatial superposition sources one x₄-geometry through G\_{μν} = (8π G/c⁴)⟨T̂\_{μν}⟩ (eq. 2.6.2), and the McGucken Sphere generated at the shared preparation event carries the Noether-conserved quantities of both branches because it is a single geometric object in 4D \[18, §1.3\].

The conversation opens on this argument and returns to it:

> “if you put a mass in a superposition of two different places, you are really faced with a very stark choice there if you do not allow gravity to also be quantum mechanical and to respond to the mass in each of these places simultaneously.”

**LTD reading.** This is a second, independently recorded statement of the argument that §8.5.5(a) quotes from the Jaimungal interview \[28\], where Vedral puts it as “the field better understand how to respond simultaneously to the object in place one and the object in place two.” The two statements agree, and the McGucken position on both is Theorem 7.2 with Remark 7.2a: the response is simultaneous because the sourcing relation (2.6.2) is evaluated on the matter expectation value, producing one geometric configuration with curvature contributions at both supports, and the conserved quantities that constrain the joint outcome are carried on the shared wavefront. Vedral reads the simultaneity as a reason to quantize the gravitational field; under dx₄/dt = ic the same simultaneity follows from the wavefront being one object, with no operator-valued metric amplitudes required (\[2, GR T19\]).

<div id="e.4-eep-in-superposition">

### E.4 The equivalence principle in superposition: “no one has done that experiment”

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** Theorem 6.2 derives the null result for any matter-wave test of the equivalence principle from two consequences of dx₄/dt = ic: the composition-independence of the Compton coupling ω_C = mc²/ℏ (\[3, Theorem 4\]; \[2, QM T4–T5\]), and the Strong Equivalence structure \[2, GR T3–T6\] under which matter couples to the geometry through T̂\_{μν} alone.

Vedral poses the question, and states the parallel with the conservation laws that Theorem 2.5.2 makes exact:

> “But a person like me looks at all of these other principles, and I think, well, wait a second, we’ve done exactly the same successfully with energy conservation, momentum conservation, all of the symmetries that we think the universe obeys are successfully absorbed into our standard model, if you like, the quantum description of all the other forces. So why not the equivalence principle? What exactly would prevent us from actually accelerating in two different ways simultaneously? But no one has done that experiment.”

He then describes, in the same conversation, the experiment that performs it:

> “a colleague of mine in Israel, Ron Fullman, has already implemented a single Stern-Gerlach… But he managed to split the Bose condensate to make it exist in two different places. In fact, to even make it accelerate differently. One branch was in free fall and the other one was stationary as a reference branch. They’re beautiful experiments. And actually, quantum mechanics works perfectly fine there, obeying simultaneously the equivalence principle in all of these.”

**LTD reading.** The apparatus described is the Quantum Galileo Interferometer of the Folman group, treated as Experiment 4 in §6 and §8.4 and read in full in Appendix D; the transcription “Ron Fullman” records Ron Folman. The measurement was published in *Science Advances* on 2 September 2026 \[50\], with Vedral among the authors, and it reports the equivalence-principle-consistent phase. Theorem 6.2 states the McGucken prediction of that null — no species-, composition-, or self-energy correction at any order, with zero free parameters — and §9.4 Table 4 records that every competing programme on the comparison either postulates the equivalence principle separately or predicts a violation requiring a tuned parameter to escape current bounds. Vedral’s question “what exactly would prevent us from accelerating in two different ways simultaneously” has, in LTD, the answer that nothing prevents it: the four-velocity budget u^μu_μ = -c² holds on each branch, the branches accumulate different proper times, and the recombined phase is the σ-image of the difference in x₄-advance.

<div id="e.5-quantize-or-gravitize">

### E.5 The quantize/gravitize dilemma and the two-tier reading

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** Under dx₄/dt = ic the matter sector and the geometric sector both descend from one expansion, so neither is forced onto the other: Theorem 7.7 holds matter-sector linearity exactly at Tier 1 and geometric-sector nonlinearity exactly at Tier 2, coupled only through the expectation value ⟨T̂\_{μν}⟩ of (2.6.2).

Vedral states the dilemma as a choice between two programmes:

> “one choice is certainly to force gravity to behave quantum mechanically, to quantize gravity. But the other one, and it seems to me Roger Penrose is fond of that approach, is that he trusts general relativity, he trusts gravity, the theory of gravity more, and he says can we gravitize or geometrize, or whatever the word would be there, quantum mechanics.”

He locates the two programmes sociologically as well:

> “you guys came up through the ranks of quantum physics. And Roger Penrose and Stephen Hawking and others in that arena came up through the general relativistic approach. And you tend to stick to your training.”

**LTD reading.** The dilemma is exhaustive only under the premise that one of the two theories must be imposed on the other. Theorem 7.7a supplies the third position: Channel A operates at both tiers with the same Lorentzian-locked character, giving an operator algebra that is linear by Stone at Tier 1 and c-number field equations that are nonlinear by Lovelock at Tier 2, with (2.6.2) as the only link between them. The verb Vedral searches for — “gravitize or geometrize, or whatever the word would be there” — names an operation that LTD does not need, since the geometry and the quantum are two readings of dx₄/dt = ic rather than two theories to be mapped onto each other. Theorem 7.6 then reads Penrose’s 1996 argument \[25\] as a confirmation of that position: the argument establishes that superposition and the equivalence principle conflict when applied to a quantizable gravitational field, and LTD predicts no quantizable gravitational field (\[2, GR T19\]).

<div id="e.6-the-mediator">

### E.6 The mediator: quantized units of geometry, or a classical background?

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The mediator of gravitationally-induced entanglement is the x₄-geometry sourced by mass-energy, the Channel B reading of dx₄/dt = ic, and its non-classicality is forced by the same imaginary unit that appears in the principle and in \[q̂, p̂\] = iℏ.

Vedral names the open question directly:

> “is gravity really all about spacetime? Which was Einstein’s initial intuition. Or should we really think of it as just another field theory, which, you know, people like Feynman, de Witt, there was that school of thought in the late ’50s and ’60s… So, are these quantum degrees of freedom that are helping us entangle these superpositions, are they really quantized units of space and time?… Or are we still gonna remain with a kind of classical background spacetime? Which we must use for some kind of convenience, but it’s not really, it’s a fiction, it’s not the fundamental entity.”

**LTD reading.** Theorem 7.3 answers the disjunction without adopting either horn, and without presupposing that the mediator obeys quantum theory — which is the premise the constructor-theoretic witness theorem of Marletto and Vedral \[22\] was designed to remove. The McGucken-expanding-wavefront field carries two distinguishable attribute pairs whose joint read-out from a single instantiation requires measurement back-action, which is the constructor-theoretic definition of non-classicality (Deutsch and Marletto \[23\]), and the argument uses only the geometric shared-identity property of the Sphere \[18, §1.3\] and the Newtonian-limit theorem \[2, GR T15\]. The geometry is therefore neither a set of quantized units of area and volume nor a classical fiction: it is the expansion itself, read through Channel B, with the metric carrying no operator-valued amplitudes.

<div id="e.7-q-numbers">

### E.7 Q numbers: why the numbers of quantum theory are complex

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The factor i in dx₄/dt = ic is the perpendicularity marker of x₄ relative to x₁x₂x₃, and Theorem B.2 derives the complex character of the amplitude from it.

Vedral prefers Dirac’s terminology to the many-worlds label:

> “I think that’s the way, and that’s what gets you into trouble with these hybrid descriptions… it really resonates with me that somehow it’s Q numbers ultimately. It’s a very abstract form of reality… But it seems to me we are stuck with these entities that are not really describable by real numbers, right? You have to assume this more dimensional quantity. And then they don’t commute.”

**LTD reading.** Dirac’s 1926 distinction between q-numbers and c-numbers named the situation without accounting for it, and Vedral’s phrasing — “you have to assume this more dimensional quantity” — states the assumption exactly. Under dx₄/dt = ic the assumption is discharged: by Theorem B.2 a wave propagating along an axis perpendicular to ℝ³, projected into the spatial slice by σ, carries a real part (the in-slice projection) and an imaginary part (the perpendicular component), so the amplitude is complex-valued because x₄ is perpendicular to space. The non-commutation Vedral names in the same breath follows from the same i through Theorem B.3, where the factor i on the right-hand side of \[q̂, p̂\] = iℏ is traced through the Minkowski signature, the path-integral phase and the momentum operator to the perpendicularity of x₄. If x₄ were a real axis, the path weights would be exp(S/ℏ), the commutator would vanish, and the physics would be classical statistical mechanics; the “more dimensional quantity” is the fourth dimension itself.

<div id="e.8-information">

### E.8 Information as the currency of reality, and the problem of the substrate

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The iterated McGucken Sphere expansion is the physical process whose Lorentzian signature-reading gives unitary Schrödinger evolution and whose Euclidean signature-reading gives the strict Second Law (Theorem 5.2.5), so the carrier of the information is the expansion of x₄.

Vedral endorses the information-theoretic programme and then names its obstruction:

> “if you look at the postulates of quantum mechanics, they really have a very strong information theoretic flavor… you’ve got John Wheeler saying it from bit. I think Weizsäcker on the German side of things, equally, I think was trying to describe reality in terms of information… it would be nice to actually find a possible information theoretic structure behind it.”

> “we think about the fact that there is no disembodied information… what would it take? And I really don’t know how to imagine that, is to go one step further and really detach information from this underlying material, substrate, if you like, and not clear how to do that.”

**LTD reading.** The obstruction he names — information requires a substrate — is answered by identifying the substrate rather than by detaching the information from it. Section 5.2.5.c reads Wheeler’s “it from bit” programme as the iterated Sphere expansion, whose two signature-readings produce information-preserving unitary evolution at the universal Hilbert-space level and information-destroying Brownian dynamics at the operationally accessible level. Remark 5.2.7 separates the three senses that the tradition conflates: global information I_G, preserved by unitary evolution; locally accessible information I_L, destroyed by the Euclidean reading; thermodynamic information I_T, increased by the strict Second Law dS/dt = (3/2)k_B/t \> 0 of \[3, Theorem 9\]. The question Vedral poses as “what comes first?” therefore has an answer within the framework: the expansion of x₄ comes first, and the three information measures are its readings.

<div id="e.9-nonlocality-and-its-test">

### E.9 Non-locality: “how do we test these things?”

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Sphere of radius r = ct centred on an event is simultaneously the light cone of relativity, the Huygens wavefront of optics, and the boundary of entanglement possibility \[18, §1.3\], so the reach of nonlocality is fixed by the expansion.

Greene proposes accepting non-locality as a feature of the world, and Vedral asks for a test:

> “the non-locality you’re talking about, I would say is a genuine non-locality. Which we could probe, you know, at certain small enough distances, for instance, it could really be that you do something here and something over there changes instantaneously, let’s say. And I think the question for me maybe would be how do we test these things? That would be really exciting to probe that.”

**LTD reading.** Section 7.2.5 supplies both the law and the test. The First McGucken Law of Nonlocality \[18, §2.1\] restricts nonlocal correlation to systems sharing a common local origin or a chain of local contacts reaching one; the Second Law \[18, §2.2\] fixes the growth rate of that capacity at c. Together they identify the Sphere of radius r = ct as the exact boundary of entanglement possibility, and the New York–Los Angeles Challenge of \[18, §3\] is the corresponding experiment: two masses with no shared local-origin chain will fail to entangle gravitationally, which is prediction §11.3(E). Section 11.10 adds the calibration Vedral’s question implies: Conjecture 11.10.2 places the Tsirelson-saturating correlation and the exact marginal flatness of no-signaling on the same Sphere, so the instantaneity is geometric while the statistics remain uninformative for signalling.

<div id="e.10-the-five-to-ten-year-prediction">

### E.10 The five-to-ten-year prediction and the six absences

</div>

**McGucken Principle anchor.** Theorem 7.1 derives the BMV entangling phase ΔΦ of (7.2) from dx₄/dt = ic, and §11.3(A)–(F) states six absences that separate LTD from the programmes agreeing with it on that phase.

Vedral’s timetable and expectation:

> “I really think that within the next five to 10 years, we will have that experiment done. And I think it will show entanglement between these masses. And so we will know that, at least at this linear regime of weak Newtonian limit, if you like, of Einstein’s theory, that it really is quantum mechanical.”

He also states the parameters: masses below a nanogram, “10 to minus even 15, 16 kilograms”, roughly 100 microns separation, micron-scale superpositions, coherence for about a second — the configuration analysed in Theorem 7.1 and §8.5.

**LTD reading.** The McGucken prediction agrees on the entanglement and on the phase, and Remark 7.10 states the difference in reading: a positive result confirms that the mediator is the x₄-geometry sourced by quantum-mechanical matter, and by the contraposition of Theorem 7.6 — with both QM linearity and the equivalence principle preserved, as the experiment will verify — gravity is a c-number sector rather than a quantum field. The six absences of §11.3 are what distinguish the two readings observationally, since the leading-order phase does not.

Of the eleven items, one has been settled experimentally since the conversation was recorded — the equivalence principle in superposition, by \[50\] — and the McGucken prediction for it was the exact null of Theorem 6.2. The remaining ten are open in the conversation and are theorems of dx₄/dt = ic in the corpus, with the Born rule the item on which Vedral states the sharpest criterion and §E.1.3 the itemised response to it.
